### Lessons

1. Translation-invariant positivity on the critical-line side is prime-blind. Three lanes reached this from different sides: Pick LMI linear in the zero measure (window-sdp), Hecke/Moebius operators as translation multipliers acting by congruence (spinor-hecke-primitive), and rank-one additive defect of commuting prime sections (multidelay). Restricting, grouping or coupling such forms adds no constraint beyond the explicit formula.
2. Where a property does separate zeta from twists, it is an identity equivalent to the target: CP(sigma), the Fejer-tapered section, and local boundedness of pole-renormalised stages are each the zero-free statement, and the implication towards zeros does not use multiplicativity.
3. Multiplicativity entered only as an equality (Hecke eigen-identity, defect order 1) or at stage level (Lee-Yang stages). Completely multiplicative controls (sign twists, unimodular twist, Beurling, zeta(s+a)) pass every such step; they fail only at the functional-equation step. DH fails the complementary step. So an R3-valid mechanism needs an inequality that uses the Euler product and the functional equation in the same step.
4. Limit steps: the self-dual conductor normalisation erases the limit (symbol -> -1); the strip topology gives Hurwitz plus a boundedness statement equal to the PNT error term. Krein-index semicontinuity is closed as a limit step. Kernel-side positivity (GHS, FK cone, log-concavity, Csordas convexity) holds for the DH kernel and gives no zero information.
5. Filter: the Liouville twist zeta(2s)/zeta(s) is an exact completely multiplicative control with zeros off the line in the strip (Re s = 1/4); finite twists carry zeta's zeros and only test statements that use the archimedean side.
6. Reliability: negative verdicts held in 8 of 8 lanes in substance; the recurring referee corrections are domain statements (whole-line L^2 vs windowed, compact support under inverse Moebius, node-uniformity of grid certificates). Proof steps labelled PROVED need the function class stated.

### Open items carried forward (not closed by round 1)

- Multivariate (polydisc, per-prime weights) Lee-Yang property: does it give a one-sided upper bound on log|E_P(s) exp(E_1((s-1) log P))| in the strip better than exp(P^(1-sigma)/log P)? (from krein-limit; the single-variable consequence is weaker than trivial)
- Size of sum over zeros of |zeta_S(1/2+i gamma)|^2 |W(i gamma)|^2 against norm(Z_S w)^2 as S grows (partial Euler products sampled at zeros; largest-value type; from spinor-hecke-primitive). Reusable code: `push/c1-spinor-hecke-primitive/plib.py`.
- LMI with on-line terms that are not sign-definite (sector matrices P+-B of stack-feasibility section 6) in the window model (from window-sdp).
- J-inner linear-fractional section in several delays with Theta(0) = M(c0)U0, c0 > 0; infinite P; DH analogue (from multidelay).
- Window defect: the commutator of the cutoff with Hecke operators (the place where a multiplicative mechanism can act on H_c); its formula is kernel-independent, so the function must enter through the test vector.
- Referee passes owed: multidelay Theorem A step (iii); missing-controls Part D (Selberg CLT, Bohr-Jessen statements); spinor Lemmas 3-5; leeyang-chain step 2.1(c) and the GHS-with-hidden-spins input. Map bookkeeping owed: section 6 items 7, 8, 11; rows P29, Q12.
- Not done: Ritz run of K[t] for the heat family at tau = -2; a sign twist with a zero in 7/8 < Re s < 1 as a control for the 7/8 mechanism.

### Lessons (round 2)

1. The round-2 design rule (Euler product and functional equation in one step) was met only as a mean value. Sampling the partial Euler product at the zeros gives the Landau-Gonek diagonal term: it needs the prime-power coefficients of -F'/F only, holds for DH at on-line and off-line zeros, and is a function of log P / log T. Two independent lanes (local and scale) agree. Partial Euler multipliers damp off-line pairs; they are not amplifiers.
2. Stage-level tools are closed for the Lee-Yang lead. Stability classes are invariant under torus rotations and a unimodular twist is a rotation; at fixed P the trivial bound is attained along heights. R2 (height-uniform) and R4 (bounded renormalised stages) conflict at fixed P. What remains is a bound at fixed t with P growing, or with P coupled to height: an upper bound for sum_{p<=P} p^(-sigma) cos(t log p) + Re E_1((s-1) log P).
3. Matrix (J-inner) freedom in the multi-delay family gives nothing: the normalisation S -> -1 plus a maximum principle forces the unipotent family. The first-moment no-go uses the functional equation and Schwarz-Pick only and holds for DH.
4. Window-local functional-equation input is not enough: zeta's lattice height translates and L(s, chi_5) at height T/5 pass any single-window test, and a window carries about 30 conditions independent of T. New control for the filter: "window translate". An attack must use the functional equation non-locally in height (Riemann-Siegel length or all heights).
5. Forms built on the cutoff (commutators with Hecke dilations included) are the Weil form on a window; they read the functional equation and the lowest off-line height, not the Euler product (DH passes, completely multiplicative Liouville fails).
6. Window-local explicit-formula identities have no error term to beat; the gap is off-line amplitude N^delta against the trivial sqrt(N) bound on short twisted prime sums, which is the zero-free statement. Only support and size of Lambda enter.
7. Reliability: verdicts held in substance in 8 of 8 lanes; the one "does not hold" is wording (an identity called exact that has a remainder). Recurring corrections: sample-size artifacts in maxima over unequal bands, error bounds attached to the wrong formula, depths of ball-arithmetic pivots not reproduced, quadrature error outside the balls.

### Results that can be combined

- Lemma B (S-periodised explicit formula, `c2-euler-at-zeros`): positivity range moves from support < log 2 to < log(2 N_S) at the price of residues of zeta'/zeta at 1 + 2 pi i k/log p. Partner: Vinogradov-Korobov gives R_S > 0 for |S| = 1 above a non-uniform threshold. Combination to test: the residue sum for |S| >= 2 bounded by o(log T) using the 7/8 architecture's input on Re s = 1 (the one mechanism with R1, R2, R3). Needs a referee pass on Lemma B and its DH form first.
- Theorem A' (`c2-jinner-multidelay-lft`, Abel-weighted first moment of zeta over poles of a positive-real Dirichlet series) with the Landau-Gonek calibration tables of `c2-euler-at-zeros-scale` (independent numerical check; DH tables need interval certification).
- Window-translate lemma (`c2-euler-cone-fe-rigidity`) plus the rotation lemma (`c2-polydisc-leeyang-bound`): together they state that a valid mechanism must read the angles t log p jointly and non-locally in height. Record both as controls in OBSTRUCTION_MAP section 2.

### Open items carried forward

- Referee Lemma B (contour shift, k = 0 residue, small divisors) and run its DH form; then the |S| >= 2 residue bound.
- Fixed-t, growing-P bound on the renormalised stage (residual of c1 Thm. D(b)); sector stability regions anchored at z_p = 1 (argued away in one line only).
- Second referee pass on Theorem A' (uniformity in k, Mellin shift, boundedness on D^inf ∩ c_0); DH conductor-5 moment formula line by line; then close STRATEGY lead 20 and map row Q12.
- Apply `MAP_PENDING.md` to `push/OBSTRUCTION_MAP.md`; add rows: rotation-invariant stage tools, window translate control, cutoff-commutator = Weil restriction.
- Engine: reconcile aboutZeros for `e1-burgers-cascade-anticausal` and `e1-euler-anchor-heat-certificate`.
- Still not done from round 1: Ritz run of K[t] for heat at tau = -2; a sign twist with a zero in 7/8 < Re s < 1 as a control for the 7/8 mechanism.

### Lessons (round 3)

1. Lemma B is closed. The pole lattice 1 + 2 pi i k/log p on the test side is the Fourier dual of the periodised prime measure; the identity is the explicit formula for a non-compactly supported test, regularised. Its enlarged support threshold log(2 N_S) is bookkeeping, not a gain. The lead rated least blocked after round 2 is a restatement.
2. Sup bounds of zeta'/zeta on a vertical line stop at the Littlewood scale: support 2 log log T for every Theta including RH. No zero-free half-plane used through absolute values reaches support c log T. What remains is a signed treatment of the lattice sum, which is row P10 (the target).
3. A fixed-point, all-P one-sided bound is a global Landau equivalence, not a window statement. It holds as a criterion for every control; the Liouville twist is its exact mirror. A fixed-height test with P <= Pmax cannot separate zeta from a control whose zero is farther than Pmax^(beta-sigma)/log Pmax.
4. No finite Euler stage carries the functional equation. About Re s = 1/2 the stage kernel has negative squares at density theta(X)/pi; the count goes to infinity along the stages and to zero in the limit, so monotone or semicontinuous limit devices (de Branges ordering, Krein-Langer counts) cannot follow it. A canonical-system stage would have to take the functional equation as an input.
5. On T^inf strip zeros are a tail datum and the functional equation a one-orbit datum. No tail-measurable, generic, almost-everywhere or rotation-invariant property reaches chi = 1. Every every-window statistic that sorts correctly is the zero-free statement.
6. A window functional-equation test reads conductor, one phase and about 30 reals per window, never the Euler product. Rigidity needs a window count of Riemann-Siegel size sqrt(T)/log T. Filter entry: any window argument with a T-independent number of windows fails against the unimodular twist (lattice translate).
7. The Riemann-Siegel half-sum splitting is unmollified Levinson. Multiplicativity enters only the size of the failure and in the adverse direction (referee correction to the lane's "only |a_n| enters").
8. First-moment no-go statements of Theorem A' type rest on Schwarz-Pick and hold for DH: they supply neither R1 nor R3.
9. Pattern across three rounds (34 lanes, 33 at score 0): every identity-type lane ends as the explicit formula on a larger test class, and every inequality step found is either the classical zero-free region or the target. All eight round-3 lanes have all four flags false.

### Families closed in round 3

- Pole-lattice test multipliers (Lemma B and its theta-family and shifted forms) as a route to an inequality.
- Lemma B times sup bounds on vertical lines, including the 7/8 half-plane as an input and S growing with T.
- One-sided bounds in P at a fixed strip point (twisted form of T2/P10).
- Truncated Euler products reflected about Re s = 1/2 as de Branges, Pontryagin or canonical-system stages.
- Tail, generic, almost-everywhere and rotation-invariant selectors on T^inf.
- Half-sum Hermite-Biehler variants at matched height (smooth cut-off, Gabcke terms, unequal lengths included); mollified pivot is row P27.
- Window functional-equation rigidity with a fixed number of windows.
- Jointly J-contractive several-delay sections (lead 20), except the three uncovered cases named in STRATEGY.

### Results that could be combined

- Helson follow-up with the multiwindow lesson: a quantitative non-tail property (sum chi(n) = c x + O(x^theta) for completely multiplicative unimodular chi) and whether it forces a zero-free region Re s > f(theta) with f(0) < 1. Finite modifications of zeta have discrepancy O(log x) and the property names chi = 1 (Klurman, recalled), so it is the one object found that is both non-tail and tied to chi = 1. Known risk: for zeta itself theta is the Dirichlet divisor-type exponent of the partial sums and the implication may run through zeta (circular); the lane must state where it does not.
- Twin-ladder follow-up with the multiwindow count: a stage whose Theta is a finite Blaschke product by construction (functional equation as input) with prime data entering at Riemann-Siegel length sqrt(T/2pi), the size at which the window test becomes rigid. This is the only form of the canonical-system gap not closed by lane 4; it must be checked against rows G10/Q10 and against Hamburger (rigidity past the crossover is not multiplicative).
- Lane 2's f(sigma_0) cap with lane 3's Landau equivalence: both say the signed lattice or P-sum is the target. No combination, one map entry (P10/T2).

### Open items carried forward

- Map: add to row T2 the twisted fixed-s form; add the fixed-window-count filter entry; record Lemma B as a restatement row; record the Euler-stage negative-square count under G10/Q10.
- Engine: closed-family patterns for rows P41, P42, G25, Q21, Q22 and for the eight round-3 families in `engine/merge_outcomes.py`; graph edges for controls WT and FM.
- Referee debts: Proposition P and the Baker-Wustholz constant (Lemma B, |S| >= 2); second reading of the DH derivation in `c3-repair-map-theorem-a/REFEREE2.md` section 3; Lemma 3 and Prop. 1(e) of the Helson lane; Klurman-Mangerel and Gonek 2012 not opened; certified infeasibility or witness at T=1e5 m=2 and T=1e6 m=8.
- Still not done from round 1: Ritz run of K[t] for heat at tau = -2; a sign twist with a zero in 7/8 < Re s < 1 as a control for the 7/8 mechanism.

None was taken verbatim. Nodes kept from the ranking or from the unused geometric list: `bridge-scattering-factorisation`, `antihermitian-strip-bound`, `af-rank-trace-inertia`, `collision-local-index`, `dbn-prime-torus-phase-closure`, `euler-fe-coupling-at-height`, `finite-support-euler-detector`.

Adaptation applied: two consecutive rounds added no referee-held partial result with score >= 2, so one deepen slot moves to explore-type work (STRATEGY section 2). No lead has a referee-confirmed score of 3 or more outside the 7/8 architecture, so the three-lane rule does not apply.

Planner pre-checks (ARGUED, not refereed; each lane must verify or correct):
- FE-symmetrised Euler stage zeta_X = P_X + chi conj(P_X) (Gonek 2012 type; the form of the canonical-system gap left open by `c3-twinladder-prime-built-chain`). Its zero measure on the line is the phase comb of phi = -2 vartheta - 2 arg P_X(1/2+it). The k = 0 term of the comb is the explicit-formula density with primes up to X. Positivity of that term on a window is the sup bound of the truncated prime sum against (1/2) log(t/2 pi), which is lesson 2 of round 3 (range X of order log^2 t). Anything new must come from the k != 0 terms or from a Rouche comparison with zeta through the approximate functional equation.
- Thin twist: for a set S of primes with counting function about x^b/log x and chi = -1 on S, 1 elsewhere, F = zeta * prod_{p in S}(1-p^-s)/(1+p^-s) has the local form (s-b)^2 near s = b if S is regular. This would be a completely multiplicative unimodular control with a zero at any chosen real b in (7/8, 1) and partial sums c x + O(x^(b+eps)). If it holds, the Helson implication can at best give zero-free Re s > theta, and the 7/8 mechanism must fail for it at a named step.

Reasons.
1. The Helson property is the one object found that is non-tail and names chi = 1. The lane must decide the converse lemma (discrepancy O(x^theta) forces a thin deviation set, hence F/zeta zero-free and pole-free on Re s > theta, which makes the implication circular) or exhibit a non-thin example.
2. The thin twist gives the 7/8 mechanism a control that keeps the Euler product and unimodularity. The step where the proof fails for it is the step that uses more than multiplicativity; it also bounds what lane 1 can give.
3. The only canonical-system form not closed in round 3: a stage that is a finite Blaschke product by construction with the functional equation as input. The crossing device is Rouche against the approximate functional equation. It also answers lead 13: the unimodular twist U passes the stage theorem and has no approximate functional equation.
4. Measures where lane 3's inequality fails: stage zeros leave the line only through collisions at stationary points of phi, a prime-only event. The run compares that event set with the actual zeros and the Rouche margin. Default server size.
5. Hecke operators compressed to the Lax-Phillips space: a norm bound p^theta + p^-theta with theta < 1/2 on resonance states would bound Re rho. Non-arithmetic groups are a structural control. Expected first obstacle: T_p does not preserve the incoming and outgoing subspaces.
6. Short-interval factorisation of Dirichlet polynomials is the main recent technique that uses multiplicativity essentially and has no map row. Decisive bookkeeping: the saving needed in averaged Hardy-Littlewood for F(alpha), alpha > 1, against what is proved.
7. One stated numerical question at a scale not sampled (more marks, larger Z). It moves part B only; the R4-directed numerical question of this round fits the default size (lane 4), so the heavy slot goes here.
8. Hold rate is above 50%, so this is the regular low-cost slot: without the pending closed-family patterns the generator keeps ranking closed combinations.

At least one lane on R4: lanes 3 and 4. Geometric program: lanes 3, 4 (twin ladder, torus, vortex), 5 (bridge, Klein surface), 6 (twin-ladder Gabor compression).

### Lessons (round 4)

1. A power-saving discrepancy hypothesis on a completely multiplicative unimodular chi compares F with zeta and nothing else (ord_F + ord_F-bar = 2 ord_zeta right of sigma_0). The proof never uses the additive spacing of the integers and passes Beurling. The one object that was non-tail and named chi = 1 gives no zero-free region not already assumed for zeta.
2. The planner's pre-check on the thin twist was wrong in two places: the zero at b is simple for the regular (floor-rule) set and double for the greedy sign set, and the measured discrepancy exponent is near b/2, not b. So real zeros of F unrelated to zeta lie right of theta on the evidence (not proved).
3. Complete multiplicativity, unimodular local parameters, continuation, the pole and density-one agreement with zeta at primes are consistent with a zero at any b in (7/8,1). The extra input of the 7/8 mechanism is lattice-translation invariance of the coefficients (every sextic twist is periodic with a Poisson dual uniform in the modulus), used in the conductor aspect of a family and never as s <-> 1-s for the target. This names R3 for the 7/8 architecture more exactly than "Euler product".
4. A stage with zeros on the line by construction carries no information in that property (holds for any non-vanishing P, including U). All content is in the comparison zeta/stage, whose Rouche form is equivalent to zero-freeness of boxes and whose prime-side form is the Landau two-sided tail bound below abscissa 1.
5. The prime-only collision event of the Euler stage is torus-invariant with positive height-independent rate at fixed log X/log T; the Rouche margin does not use the Euler product (DH passes at comparable rates). Measured to T = 1e9, torus model to 1e20.
6. On the continuous spectrum Hecke operators are functions of the wave group. The Lax-Phillips framework is the same for every inner scattering function; all arithmetic is inside phi.
7. The Matomaki-Radziwill family proves statements whose conclusion class is closed under linear combination (DH passes) and whose savings are capped at log powers. Multiplicativity essential to a proof is not R1 unless it is essential to the conclusion class.
8. The mark Gram ratio is explained by the common-column overlap (Lemma O) and is not specific to the theta coefficients. The correct random control is one phase per column; n1/n2's per-(mark, cofactor) control understates the ratio, and n2's constant fails for wide slots.
9. Pattern across four rounds (42 lanes, 40 at score 0, two at score 1): no lane has R4; every R1 obtained is at stage level or relative to zeta. All four device nodes the planner named for crossing (Rouche stage, collision index, Hecke compression, Helson discrepancy) are closed.

### Families closed in round 4

- Discrepancy-of-partial-sums hypotheses on completely multiplicative unimodular chi as a source of zero-free regions (relative to zeta; Beurling passes).
- Functional-equation-symmetrised Euler stages (Gonek type) with Rouche or Hurwitz comparison, including Fejer weights and the approximate functional equation as re-expression.
- Stage collision / local-index events as carriers of zero information (torus-invariant, positive rate).
- Hecke operators compressed to the Lax-Phillips space, and Hecke norm bounds on resonance states in the weighted-L2 family.
- Ramare / Matomaki-Radziwill factorisation and pretentious-distance statements as every-window or one-sided inputs (MV-CAP, tail type).

Not closed: norms outside the one weight family in the Lax-Phillips lane; the non-locality step 4 of the Rouche lane; which of the two estimates of Proposition D fails for THIN.

### Results that could be combined

- THIN with the sextic tools (`tmp/sextic`): mean square over m of the completed cubic theta row with weight eps_K against weight 1 at two or three scales Z, b = 0.95. Growth like Z^(2(b-11/12)) puts the failure on the low estimate (row reflection). This decides which half of the 7/8 mechanism carries the lattice-periodicity input and is a default-size computation.
- Lesson 3 with the Helson lane: the Helson hypothesis (partial sums of chi) is additive-blind; the 7/8 input is periodicity of twists in the conductor aspect. A hypothesis on chi stated for all twisted sums sum chi(n) e(an/q), uniformly in q (a Poisson dual in the family), is the form not covered by lane 1's Landau argument or by Beurling. It must be checked against the multiwindow lesson (Hamburger: rigidity that is not multiplicative) and against THIN (non-periodic by construction).
- Kloss overlap (Lemma O, column-phase control) with the unexplained K4a term (regression 0.42-0.59 against 0.98 for its random control): a structured off-principal term of overlap size in a neighbouring system; frontier part B only.
- By-product (D3) of the Ramare lane (isolated off-line zeros bounded by T^(2-2 sigma) log T from one explicit formula at X = T) is a density statement; compare with the density literature before any use.

Three lanes (2, 3, 4) attack that statement by independent approaches.

Planner pre-checks (ARGUED, not refereed; each lane must verify or correct):
- Twisted Helson hypothesis, sum chi(n) e(an/q) = c delta_q x + O(q^A x^theta) for all q up to x: Parseval over the circle gives a sup error of at least (x(1-|c|^2))^(1/2), so theta < 1/2 forces chi = 1 (identification); for theta >= b the THIN control passes (eps = 1 * h, h supported on numbers built from the thin set). The range 1/2 <= theta < b with q <= x^delta is what lane 5 has to decide.
- Recorded inconsistency: round-4 lesson 2 reports a measured THIN discrepancy exponent near b/2 with a zero at b, while Theorem 1 of the Helson lane places all zeros right of sigma_0 among zeta's. One of the two (the fitted exponent, or the identification of sigma_0 with the discrepancy exponent) needs correction. Lane 5, test 1.
- Shift identity 1 = sum_{k>=1} (-1)^(k-1) C(s+k-1, k) zeta(s+k) (integers closed under n -> n+1) expresses zeta in the strip through values right of 3/2. It is the Mellin transform of (e^x - 1) * 1/(e^x - 1) = 1, linear, holds for DH with shift 5, and its terms cancel from size about e^|t|. Common wall part 2; no lane.
- Angular family lambda^(6k) over Q(sqrt -3): the family sum is supported on rays (rational integers, count Z^(1/2) among norms <= Z), i.e. coherent exponent 1/2. Its epsilon factor is i^(6k) times a Bessel kernel; if that is a character of k the ap-order-two obstruction (no absorption of mu) repeats. Lane 3, first test.
- Stage families with both zero-freeness and an integer-proved strip limit (smooth-number partial sums with x = y^u): zero-freeness holds only for u > y^(1-sigma) by a size argument valid for every twist, convergence only for u bounded. Same gap as `c1-leeyang-krein-limit` Theorem D; no lane.

Its answer says whether the periodicity input sits in the row reflection (low estimate) or in the high comparison, which decides whether lane 2's object (reflection) is the binding one. Heavy slot: four or more scales over a factor 64 and two values of b need more than the default size; no other stated question would change the next allocation.
2. The floor 1 - h/r with h <= 1 was derived for a system that never reflects the target. An exact LP with one more variable decides whether the functional equation of the target moves the floor. Expected outcome: h <= 1 stays, as a proved row; a vertex below 5/6 would be the first R4-directed gain inside an R1-R3 mechanism.
3. A coherent set of exponent 1/2 exists in a family the map does not list. The absorption identity is a finite check on the epsilon factor. If it fails, the result is a general row covering every family whose epsilon factor is a character of the family parameter.
4. Row Z15 is ARGUED and its order-2 branch is a heuristic. Either it becomes a proof (family closed properly) or the inconsistent region of the reduced system contains a vertex with a weaker needed input.
5. Named as combinable in round 4. The pre-check leaves one open range; the lane also resolves an inconsistency between two round-4 records, which lanes 1 and 6 depend on.
6. THIN now carries the R3 reading of the only R1-R3 mechanism while resting on a theorem cited from memory and a walk by hypotheses. The positive direction (abscissa as a function of the periodicity exponents) gives a consistency test that can falsify either THIN or the walk.
7. All four device nodes the planner named in round 4 are closed and 54 of the generator's top 60 rest on a keyword. The lane tests device types that never appeared in the table and returns nodes for the graph.
8. Engine debts were carried two rounds; closed devices still rank in the top 60. Hold rate is above 50%, so this is the regular repair slot.

At least one lane on R4: lanes 2, 3, 4 (effective order 2 by three approaches) and 7. Geometric program: lane 3 (torus, spinor, Klein, twin ladder), lane 7 item (c) (bridge symbol S) and item (g) (annulus, elliptic double cover, Klein phase transport). Heavy: lane 1 only. Server use: lanes 1 and 5; all others local.

### Lessons (round 5)

1. The signal constant of a probe in the Prop. 2.1 format is an invariant of the probe (Lemma U). Re-expressing a probe can lower the proved low exponent only; h does not move. A route to h > 1 needs a different probe, and the only region where the ledger shows one (lx < l) is where the reduced system is inconsistent with an unidentified false input.
2. A coherent set of exponent 1/2 must be coherent for every (s, c) pair. In abelian families (characters injective in the row parameter) the only such row is the trivial character, i.e. the Moebius sum. mu is absorbed only through a nontrivial weight-1 cocycle of the epsilon factor (Jacobi sums, order >= 3).
3. A comparison system reaching abscissa 1/2 sits at a critical point (h = 3) where its low input is equivalent to GRH for the family; TRO6 at large b on the compensated ray is a restatement of the conclusion. This is inside r3's transcribed model and not refereed against the manuscript.
4. Modulus-uniform additive-twist hypotheses on chi are an identification (strict form) or are passed by THIN (general form). Periodicity of twists separates zeta from THIN and Beurling but supplies no inequality.
5. Ask what a device reads before work: frequencies only, discrete special values, sizes of local parameters, or the zero set itself. Each class has a named control that passes (any Dirichlet series; interpolation twin; unimodular twist; DH). The only pairing failing both DH and sign twists at non-identity steps is Vinogradov-Korobov, capped at (log t)^(-2/3).
6. A total mean-square exponent cannot detect a component that is 1% of the total; regression on a reference weight with a large Mertens sum does. The component found is linear in the Mertens-type sum and is not specific to multiplicative weights, so it is not an R1 signal. The measured rows do not decide Proposition D; only the probe (6.1) does.
7. The replayed steps of the 7/8 manuscript use the target only as a unimodular completely multiplicative scalar; the single target-specific hypothesis is Lemma 5.8 (finite-ray character). THIN still rests on a Hoheisel-type theorem that was not located.
8. Pattern across five rounds (50 lanes, 48 at score 0): R4 has not been obtained by any lane. Three independent approaches to effective order 2 inside the only R1-R3 mechanism returned the floor (5/6 at r = 6) or an input equivalent to the conclusion.

### Families closed in round 5

- Re-expression of the same probe in the 7/8 ledger (target functional equation, Voronoi, AFE, height aspect): Lemma U plus exact LP.
- Abelian coherent families at effective order 2 (angular lambda^(6k), ring-class, anticyclotomic, quadratic): no weight-1 epsilon cocycle, no absorption of mu.
- Additive-twist (major-arc) discrepancy hypotheses on completely multiplicative chi: identification or passed by THIN. Closes the Helson family with round 4.
- Devices: Baker linear forms, finite Turan power sums, p-adic/Iwasawa interpolation, tensor-power squeeze, Carleson/corona on the bridge symbol, strip Dirichlet-to-Neumann phase identity, sum-product (by argument).
- Heat family as an object for Suzuki's determinant criterion (no causal kernel for tau < 0).

Closed inside a model only (not a closed family): long prime marks to h = r/2 (`c5-marks-circularity-order2`).

### Lessons

1. Section 4.0 (effective order 2 inside the 7/8 architecture) has an obstruction on each of its attack items: family side (double covers, item 2), probe side (unreflected marks, item 3), the h = 3 vertex (item 4), and probes of higher degree in the target (pair lane). Lemma U (three derivations) plus degree-one homogeneity of the estimate system cover every re-expression and every n-fold probe. What is left under 4.0 is derivation work, not a new vertex: the uncompensated high side from manuscript (12.5)/(16.1), and the compensated principal signal below Re s = 3/5.
2. New structural fact for the format: a Prop. 2.1 probe must carry its normaliser H. For sextic rows H contains L(6s-3, infinite-order character) with zeros in [7/12, 2/3), so the format's unconditional conclusion stops at 2/3. ORDER-2 needs the added requirement "normaliser has no zeros in Re s > 1/2".
3. In the unreflected range the row norm K + D is itself a zero-free statement about the target (Hasse-Davenport turns the dual coefficients into mu times a Hecke character). Norm inputs and the conclusion are not independent there. Random rows pass the flatness measurement, so flatness is not an arithmetic test.
4. The periodicity question (which half of Proposition D fails for THIN) cannot be settled by enumeration: the second sixth-power frequency enters at Z >= 2^36. It is now a paper question: LIOU-HIGH (high comparison for the Liouville weight via 1/L(x, liou chi) = L(x, chi)/L(2x, chi^2)).
5. Sign information and size information do not compose: the bridge reads the pair (rho, 1 - conj rho) by sign, and its product has size Z for every beta, so it cannot feed a size comparison.
6. Coefficient squares are positive without multiplicativity and invariant under unimodular twists; multiplicativity enters only through factorisation, which is evaluated at the leading pole on Re s >= 1. Quadratic positivity is closed for pointwise outputs.
7. Square realisations: counting tests are defeated by sub-selection; fixed Hecke rules fail because a zero's eigencharacter is that of an Eisenstein series; the no-gos hold for all controls and supply no R3.
8. Process: one STRATEGY lead was stale (already run). Check push/ and engine/outcomes/ for an existing lane before allocating. Scripts run from the repo root write relative outputs there; lanes must pass absolute output paths. Recalled sources changed labels twice when opened; "recalled" stays a debt.

- Double covers of any rank as a source of a weight-0 (shift 0) cocycle through local data; twist families of any GL(n) form (given the recalled Gelfand-Tsetlin recipe; referee narrowed the claim).
- Probes of higher degree in the target inside the Prop. 2.1 ledger (pair, n-fold, unequal rows); the bridge pair as a size input.
- Long prime marks at h = 3 as an input weaker than GRH (closed inside the model; (R) => GRH open).
- Square intertwiners with a fixed Hecke rule (GL_2 cuspidal target over Q) and the Casimir class.
- Degree-2 positive forms with pointwise output (device `map-open-9`, pointwise half).

Not closed: global Whittaker constants of a genuine cusp form on the double cover of GL(3); density (family) quadratic forms (MV-CAP applies); rank >= 2 compact-at-infinity squares without a Hecke rule (no R1); model vertices with 1 < h <= 3.

This decides whether any vertex with 1 < h <= 3 exists and whether (S) or H0' is the false input.

Outcome would be a theorem: row bound (5.36) is false for a non-periodic completely multiplicative unimodular weight, which locates R3 of the 7/8 mechanism in the low half.

### Lessons

1. "h > 1 by marks" is circular, on three independent lines. (a) Manuscript algebra: marks are coherent at sixth-power frequencies, so the marked-row energy contains the squared signal (Lemma C, argued). (b) F_q[T] exact arithmetic: the h > 1 vertices are real where the Riemann hypothesis holds, and the row bound they need bounds the target's Moebius sum and fails for the Moebius-twisted weight of the same modulus. (c) Estimate system: the cone vertices (t, t, 2t-1) have one input that implies the conclusion. The round-6 attribution (false input = (S) or H0') is reversed: the false input at lx < l is the K + D energy bound.
2. The abscissa cap 1 - 1/r of the Proposition 2.1 format needs neither lx >= l nor the target: the K-loss is the Hilbert-Schmidt lower bound of the reflected row operator. No large-sieve (operator-norm) improvement passes it. What remains under ORDER-2 is one hypothesis: a vector-specific mean square of reflected, completed marked theta rows for QP > Z (E-K). It is numerically decidable with the stand-alone tools in tmp/sextic.
3. Periodicity (R3 of the 7/8 mechanism) sits in the low half as the absence of one term kappa |m_w|^2, kappa ~ Z^(7/6). That term is a Hermitian form in the weight and is present for non-multiplicative weights, so the low half carries neither R1 nor R4. For Liouville the statement is one written contour argument away from a theorem; THIN needs conductor-uniform bounds.
4. First-moment signs do not separate zeta from DH in any section class (polydisc, shell, gamma-coupled). The forced sign of Theorem A' is Harnack on the polydisc and does not survive non-integral frequencies or the gamma ratio.
5. The 7/8 input and the anticausal Hankel block read opposite ends of the off-line pole set (largest real part against smallest; growth exponent against count). Family sums do not couple members. Joining the geometric program to the 7/8 input through traces or indices gives the input restated or a density.
6. The Landau-Siegel device uses periodicity as reciprocity (chi is the Frobenius of a field), which is a different use from lattice-translation invariance in the 7/8 proof. It works in the conductor aspect only; the height aspect n^(-it) is control U.
7. Process. (a) `merge_outcomes.py` now reads an optional `engineVerdict` in a record; the two round-6 records whose referee did not hold carry `engineVerdict: open`. (b) The six earlier records with `map-open-1` as only node now list `[map-open-1, out-<slug>]`, so the open device is no longer rejected wholesale; the generator has not been rerun. (c) Devices: `laurent-shell-gamma-section` closed, `landau-siegel-mechanism` floored, `function-field-order2-vertex` kept open (compensated probe). (d) Two lanes disclosed rule deviations: `c7-ff-order2-vertex-exact` (18 requested cores against 16 for about ten minutes; two local runs outside memcap), `c7-uncompensated-high-side-normaliser` (one ssh connection running `true`). (e) c5 `tiers_fe2.log` rows Y2, Y6 do not reproduce with the current script.

- Sections of the prime chain on a Reinhardt shell or with the gamma one-port, tested by first moments (holds for DH). Not covered: P(t) growing like a power of t.
- Family sums or family-averaged Krein indices of bridge forms under the 7/8 input (finite pole sets).
- The Landau-Siegel determinant device as a crossing device beyond width c/log q or in the height aspect.
- Inside the 7/8 architecture: h > 1 by uncompensated marks (conditional on Lemma C; exact in F_q[T] at N <= 4); operator-norm estimates below 1 - 1/r.

Not closed: (E-K) vector-specific mean square of reflected marked theta rows for QP > Z; the compensated probe (12.5) in F_q[T]; THIN row bound (T1, T2); a bridge formed from a single probe row B_m.

### Results that could be combined

- `c8-ek-ff-reflected-rows-exact` (compute, heavy, precise-obstruction, score 0, holds in reduced form): F_q[T], q = 7, 13, squarefree unreflected marked rows (reflected completed rows not run). Row energy = diagonal + squared signal; Weil targets at the diagonal in the bulk, and so are random-phase, random-sign, multiplicative-sign and two-character vectors. Proved: energy / diagonal >= B_0 at dual length 0, so the row bound implies the Moebius bound of the weight. Failure for targets beyond h = 3 is argued from 5 small cases. Compensated probe: h > 1 survives, low input unchanged.

(E-K) is a mean value of 1/L over a family containing the target; under GRH for the family it holds for X >= ZP (outline). Window Z/P < X < ZP open. The Dunn-Radziwill extremal vector is not the probe's configuration.
- `c8-eh-nonprincipal-row-cancellation` (deepen, precise-obstruction, score 0, not held): NP = I - I_1 exactly; a bound on NP is necessary and sufficient for the conclusion given the low estimate (necessity, not circularity). Termwise and norm treatments closed in the model (13/15; 31/36 with E-K). Row cancellation in F_q[T] at r <= 4 appears for every weight. Target-specific treatment undecided: (E-H) stays an open escape.

Liouville rows: N = D + P + Rem, P >= 0 of exact order M^(5/6) X^(1/3), abs(Rem) <= C X^(1+eps) relative to a fixed-twist form of Blomer-Goldmakher-Louvel Cor. 1.4. Liouville analogue of (5.36) fails for 7m/8 < n < 5m/4. THIN conditional on an unknown zero-free half-plane for twists of zeta_F.

Lemma C's (C1) holds only relative to the unproved high half (HC) and beta* > 2/3. F_q[T]: E = S + Rem, Rem >= 0, exact. Marks branch reduced to (E-K), not closed.

Scoreboard after the round: headline 1 of 4 (`c1-leeyang-krein-limit`; missing R2, R3, R4); no lane with R4; 74 lanes, hold rate 86.5%; tag-pair families 30,135: closed 1,606, tried and not closed 461, untried 28,068; lemmas decided 30; PROVED claims broken 8; three-referee statements 1 (`c8-row-energy-squared-signal`, an obstruction statement, not about zeros).

### Lessons

1. (E-K) is not an operator-norm statement and not an independent input. Over Z[omega] the row mean square of the probe vector is, exactly, a mean value of 1/L(1+u, .) over the order-6 twist family of the target (Poisson in the complete row variable; theta coefficient times Gauss sum = Moebius times a ray character). In F_q[T] the same quantity is diagonal + squared signal, and a diagonal-level bound implies the target's own Moebius bound. Two independent lines; both leave (E-K) undecided, of zero-free type.
2. The row energy carries no R3. Random-phase, random-sign and multiplicative-sign vectors sit at the diagonal like the Weil targets (the mean over random phases equals the diagonal exactly). The pre-registered control "random vector shows the K-loss" was wrong: operator-norm sizes need an aligned vector. Controls for a mean-square hypothesis must be aligned or Moebius-type vectors.
3. Necessity is not circularity. "A bound on NP is equivalent to the conclusion given the low estimate" holds for every proved high-side input of the manuscript too. The referee rejected the closure of (E-H) on this ground. What is closed for (E-H) is the termwise and the norm treatment (floors 13/15, 31/36 in the model); cancellation over rows is a property of Poisson duality and appears for non-multiplicative weights at r <= 4.
4. The round-7 "coherent term" (Lemma C) is the Cauchy-Schwarz step of Proposition 2.1 read backwards. It gives a lower bound for the row energy and no conclusion without the high half (HC), which map row Z24 allows only for beta* > 2/3. Cite Lemma C only as "(C1) relative to (HC), beta* > 2/3" or through the F_q[T] identity.
5. Liouville against THIN: for the Liouville weight mu * liou = 1 puts L in the numerator of each dual-frequency series, so a second moment bounds the remainder (theorem relative to Blomer-Goldmakher-Louvel). Every other unimodular multiplicative weight, THIN included, has 1/L there and needs a zero-free half-plane for the whole twist family. The periodicity half of the 7/8 mechanism is a weight-blind Hermitian form: no R1, no R4.
6. A single theta or Eisenstein-type row has its unitary axis at Re w = 1 of the L-function in its coefficients. A bridge on one row is on the right-of-1 side of the R4 wall. The target appears as an L-function only after the sum over rows, and family sums were closed in round 7. The bridge / 7/8 junction is closed on both sides.
7. On the modular surface the controls U, THIN and sign twists fail only by lack of automorphy. The sharper control is EIS-w: E(z, w') off resonance, which keeps the group, functional equation, Euler product and Hecke algebra. A device there must read phi(w) = 0; under RH resonances sit at Re w = 3/4, off the unitary axis, where invariant-measure tools do not apply.
8. Process. (a) Three lanes did not append their line and left `lanes_line.json` in the lane directory; the learn step wrote all eight lines from the records. (b) Two lanes wrote a same-agent check into REFEREE.md before the independent referee; the independent report was placed first. (c) A PROVED lemma about a control object must include a numeric check that the object is not identically zero. (d) 2 of 6 advance predictions of the heavy lane missed; one server unit was killed by its own memory limit and dropped, limit not raised. (e) Devices (`engine/curated/mk_devices.py`, run; `build_graph.py` and `generate.py` not rerun by this step): `single-row-bridge` closed; (E-K), (E-H), function-field vertex kept open with narrowed evidence. Records of lanes whose device stays open carry `engineVerdict` partial or open and list `[device node, out-<slug>]`.

### Lessons

1. The (E-K) window is closed for absolute-value methods and fails in the model. Over Z[omega], with more dual frequencies than columns the 1/L form is dominated by its own diagonal (N/X) D, so family GRH, density estimates and second moments stop at N^(1+eps). In F_q[T], where the family RH is a theorem, Weil targets exceed the diagonal by a growing factor above dual length 3N. Two independent lines, same direction. The pre-registered branch for round 10 is "fails": ORDER-2 through (E-K) closes at the 13/15-type floors. Caveats: the model result is N = 2 only; the Z[omega] float screen shows no target excess (ratio at most 1.007), which the model result does not match, and the difference (number field against model, or sizes) is unexplained.
2. (E-H) is closed in the model. The non-principal row sum is a linear functional of the weight with a kernel that does not depend on the target; seeded control classes show the same cancellation exponent as the targets. With lesson 1, both escapes from the ORDER-2 linear program inside the 7/8 architecture are closed or failing in the model.
3. The format ceiling is exact resonance, not smallness. The normaliser obstruction can be moved only by a family closed under eta -> (alpha eta)^6; the maps 6s - 3 and 4 - 6s leave the window [4/7, 3/5], so even with every format hypothesis granted the sextic format does not reach 1/2. The hypotheses for infinite-order targets have no source.
4. Sum rules return densities. A positive-term sum rule gives "no bound states" only if its coefficient side vanishes, and then its limit step is the target. THIN is the first control for any coefficient condition in the delay variable: thin sign changes keep smoothness-in-delay and place a zero right of the line.
5. Stratification by a divisibility label is inclusion-exclusion: it recombines sublattice copies of the total and has no saving of its own. A ratio statistic must be read against its own random-sign control (here decaying like 1/log Q), not against 0.
6. Seeded control classes with a pre-registered rule decide what single-weight comparisons cannot: a gap of 0.1 to 0.2 in a cancellation exponent is inside the seed spread.
7. Record. With one referee per lane no line of Statement O is PROVED. The joint clause "a proof must read all of these in one step" is not supported; only 13 separate conditional statements are. Debts that a lane cannot discharge (closed-access sources) are to be recorded as "relative to".
8. Process. (a) All eight lanes left `lanes_line.json` in the lane directory; the learn step wrote the lines from the records. (b) Reconciled here: `outcomes/c8-hecke-entropy-resonance-rigidity.json` (second referee holds the corrected statement; engineVerdict closed) and the "signature (2,2)" wording (correction field on the `c8-ek-theta-second-moment-formula` lane line and outcome). (c) NOT done by this step, for the planner: `engine/curated/mk_devices.py` still has `nonprincipal-row-cancellation`, `vector-specific-row-mean-square` and `function-field-order2-vertex` as open, and `positive-term-sum-rule` (proposal in `push/c9-szego-sum-rule-prime-cascade/DEVICE_NODE.json`) is not registered; the engine was not rerun (`merge_outcomes.py`, `build_graph.py`, `generate.py`); map rows Z15d, Z24b and MAP_ROWS_PROPOSED.md are not applied. The outcome record for (E-H) carries engineVerdict closed on node `eh-nonprincipal-row-cancellation`. (d) One lane wrote three JSON files to the repo root and moved them back; `b1_blocks.json` and `checks.json` in the repo root are untracked and were not touched. (e) Server directory `~/rh-push/c9-tro6-interstratum-cancellation` (477 MB) can be deleted.

### Families now closed (added this round)

- (E-H) non-principal row cancellation, in the F_q[T] model: linear functional with target-independent kernel; no target excess at r <= 5 (second same-kind obstruction; measured, pre-asymptotic).
- (E-K) window Z/P < X < ZP by absolute-value methods (argued and measured over Z[omega]); diagonal-level window bound fails in F_q[T] at N = 2 above dual length 3N (exact, pre-registered). One bounded check outstanding: completed rows at N = 6, run (6, 9, 2), q = 7.
- Positive-term sum rules on the prime stub chain (Szego, Killip-Simon, Simon-Zlatos, H^2 point evaluation): controls U and THIN; one referee.
- TRO6 lead 17 by squarefull stratification of the signed c-sum (Lemma I, Proposition E).
- Hecke recurrence / measure rigidity on Eisenstein resonance states: closed on the corrected statement, control EIS-w.
- Normaliser ladder as a route to 1/2: stops in [4/7, 3/5] even with all format hypotheses.

### Results that could be combined

1. Slot decomposition (c9-mv6-window-statement, Lemmas S1, S2) + the F_q[T] exact code (c9-mv6-ff-window-exact): split the model row energy by pairs of marked primes and compare Cross with the squared signal B_coh. This would say whether the model excess is the cross term and explain the disagreement between the model (excess up to 20.6) and the Z[omega] screen (none).
2. Completed-row lesson (one squared signal per cube step, shortest cofactor dominant) + the manuscript signal of section 7.2: the bounded run (6, 9, 2) at q = 7 tests the argued threshold r > 2N - 3.
3. Referee's thin-prime-deletion control (positivity kept, real zero b in (1/2, 1)) + Statement O line 1 and line 5: gives a zero-mode control with Lambda >= 0, replacing the unopened Beurling source; needs a written construction and a referee.
4. Lemma U1 (pointwise, qualitative form of (2.2)) + the question whether the format can be posed for alpha^6 chi (MS (7.10)-(7.13) with an angular factor): the only step that could lower the ceiling 2/3, to 3/5 at best.

### Lead that looks least blocked

Inside the architecture: none toward a crossing; the remaining items are bounded checks (combinables 1 and 2) and the format question for infinite-order targets (ceiling 2/3 to 3/5, relative to the format). For TRO6 (part B only): the dual form on squarefree n and Type I on average over the level are untried stand-alone routes. Outside the architecture no device with R4 is on file after the sum-rule closure; the device class not covered by any closed row is "coefficient side identically zero plus a semicontinuous limit step", and that limit step is the R4 statement itself. The round-9 reading is that the attack slots leave ORDER-2, and that a new device type must be chosen by the planner against the control list THIN, U, EIS-w and thin prime deletion before any computation.

If equal: the model's h > 1 is carried by the Hodge index theorem, and the number-field missing input is named as an intersection form (state "model vertex with h > 1 and a named unproved input"). If not: the squared signal is a family average (monodromy invariants), which is a mean-value object, and the row is written. Controls: aligned and Liouville-twisted vectors; random vectors.
8. For each: which closed family or device names the result as its missing tool (STRATEGY 3.1, map section 6). Output: a table (result, status, family reopened or none). Search terms are generic; no project detail goes into a query. Raise: none; a named input reopens a family in round 11.

Not launched, with reason: a height-coupled contractive section with P(t) a power of t (named in `c7-laurent-shell-gamma-section`): the draw at P = t^theta has mean square of order (theta log t)^2 against the reserve (1/2) log t, so the one-sided bound fails on a positive proportion of heights (STRATEGY 3.1 item 4, sup bounds). Lorentzian polynomials of degree >= 3: the class reads nonnegative coefficients and returns log-concavity (necessary conditions of Turan type), no zero location. Bost-Connes / KMS states: read set is real beta > 1. Potential-theoretic limit steps (convergence of log|F_P| in L^1_loc): equivalent to the zero-free statement by the Riesz measure, same kind as STRATEGY 3.1 item 1. Truncated Fourier expansions of E*(z, s) with exact functional equation: row G17 (cosine dominance, Euler-blind). Combinable 4 (format for alpha^6 chi): ceiling 3/5 at best and no source for its hypotheses. Global Whittaker constants on the double cover of GL(3): kept pending; ORDER-2 attack slots are closed this round.

## Round 10 results

Learn step 2026-11-19. Records: `campaign/round10_records.json` (referee notes truncated in the input; full text in each lane's REFEREE.md). Script: `campaign/learn_r10.py`. Eight lanes, seven referee holds (each in a narrowed or corrected form; one referee per lane), one not held (`c10-repair-record-referee-r9`: one PROVED label of Statement O not confirmed as written). Every score 0, no statement about zeros, no ALERT.

- `c10-ff-slot-cross-decomposition` (combine, partial-result, score 0, holds with corrections): F_q[T], q = 7, 13, N = 2, 3, long marks, 58 configurations, exact. The excess of the row energy over the diagonal is the cross term over pairs of distinct marked primes (slot-diagonal = diagonal up to O(N q^(N - lam/2)), proved via Weil). Target a at q = 7 (2,12,1): e = 20.639, Cross/D = 19.639, B/D = 19.426. The registered rule "excess = coherent cross" is not awarded: it fails for the second target (n = 5.31), one U seed and mu; n <= 1.5 holds in 583 of 596, the registered c_perp band in 593 of 596. Post hoc (d = 1, 7 rows): a second squared signal in the vector's own character. Target not separated from random multiplicative signs (50 of 51). The round-9 model / Z[omega] disagreement is geometry (screen at P = Z^(1/3); model excess needs marks of length at least 3.5N), argued.
- `c10-ff-completed-rows-692` (compute, heavy, precise-obstruction, score 0, holds narrowed): pre-registered run (6, 9, 2), q = 7, made. Completed energy/diagonal 1.688 (a), 2.002 (b), 5.905 (trivial) against squarefree 0.567, 0.597, 0.862; random vectors 0.80 to 1.07. The literal band is met on the primary vector only through offsetting deviations (cube term 1.377, not 0.8; squarefree part at 0.57 of its diagonal; cross terms -0.23). The completed-row law with exact inputs fails (coherent count off by 15.7 and 0.088). Cube term: half n = l^2 (principal direction), half squarefree n (sum over m exactly zero). One configuration; no exponent.
- `c10-thin-deletion-zero-mode-control` (combine, partial-result, score 0, holds with a repaired Lemma 0): relative to a classical short-interval prime theorem, deleting a greedy prime set of density t^(b-1)/log t gives F = zeta(s)(s - b)W(s) with Lambda_F >= 0 and a simple zero at b in (1/2, 1). Density one half gives a square-root branch point, not a zero. Finite data do not enclose the zero. Device read (argued): 14 pass, 20 do not; count not confirmed against the table.
- `c10-repair-record-referee-r9` (repair, partial-result, score 0, NOT held as written): Statement O has a second referee file for 12 lines. Standing: PROVED lines 6 (without KER), 8 (no floor), 9, and line 3 only for the inputs {EP, POS, FE}; MEASURED 1; ARGUED 7. Joint clause withdrawn. Map 166 -> 180 rows. Round-9 records carry correction fields in 7 of 8. Engine rerun: 1,640 nodes, 8,855 edges, 40,671 generated, 10,311 scored.

Measured at 1e6, 1e7: no saving at resolution 0.01 to 0.03 (eight decision differences above -0.02). The one firing of the rule is removed by projecting out the Theorem P polar direction (chosen after the rule fired); remainder compatible with zero, not shown to be zero. Two non-decision cases unexplained. No eta > 0.

In genus 2 it fails (0 of 80; 0 of 624) and the index theorem bounds the signal from above only. No new input named.

Scoreboard after the round: headline 1 of 4 (`c1-leeyang-krein-limit`; missing R2, R3, R4); no lane with R4; 90 lanes, hold rate 87.8%; tag-pair families 35,511: closed 1,867, tried and not closed 511, untried 33,133; lemmas decided 43; PROVED claims broken 10; three-referee statements 1 (not about zeros).

### Lessons

1. The round-9 caveat is removed: the F_q[T] model and the Z[omega] screen do not disagree. The model excess is a cross term over pairs of distinct marked primes and needs marks of length at least 3.5N; the screen ran at P = Z^(1/3), where the predicted term is 1.5e-4 to 3.2e-4 of the diagonal against resolution 0.01 (argued). The excess is not target-specific: random multiplicative signs are not separated from the target (50 of 51).
2. The row energy carries more than one squared signal. B_coh alone under-counts (second target: 2.16 of 7.47), and the completed row adds a cube term that is half normaliser (n = l^2, principal direction) and half a part on non-principal frequencies that the principal probe does not see. The round-8 coherent count does not predict vectors with cancellation.
3. A pre-registered total can be met by offsetting errors (cube term 1.72 times the stated size, squarefree part at 0.57, cross terms -0.23). Register the exact decomposition, not only the total. Likewise a rule "exponent below the seeded controls" fires on a decaying coherent term (polar term, class mean): the removal of those components must be registered before the fit.
4. Lead 17 (TRO6 signed c-sum) has three same-kind records (e2, c9, c10): stratification, the dual on squarefree n and Type I over the level are re-bracketings of one trilinear sum. At reachable sizes the exponent is resolved to 0.01 to 0.03, far coarser than the Q^(-eta/80) that TRO6 needs; more runs of this form decide nothing.
5. Reciprocity identities act on the L-value variable (n or the spectral parameter) and leave the mollifier twist l fixed or turn it into a level. The saving the n4 window needs is cancellation in l. Weights that have a dual family replace Katok-Sarnak positivity by functional-equation positivity, which E_a with real a also satisfies.
6. In genus 1 every squared signal is an intersection-form square for an algebraic reason (deg[m] = m^2), so the identity separates nothing; the Hodge index theorem enters only as an upper bound (the Weil bound). A model "square" carries positivity content only for targets of genus >= 2, where the Moebius signal is not trace-linear.
7. The thin-deletion control (Lambda >= 0, coefficients 0/1, pole at 1, real zero at b) together with THIN covers degree-one Euler-product read sets that omit the functional equation and periodicity. The devices it does not pass read the functional equation, periodicity with unimodular parameters, an attached object, or positivity with zeta's gamma side. Its zero comes from the theorem, not from data.
8. Public follow-ups over five weeks stay inside the same probe format and normaliser. The new inputs are arbitrary-coefficient inequalities (large sieve, density): control-blind, part B only. The claimed frontier figure 0.874462 (conditional, unverified) is below the brief's 0.874887; the scoreboard part B was not changed.
9. Process. (a) Lane `c10-ff-slot-cross-decomposition` reports that it sent SIGTERM to PID 80786, a waiter script `queue3.sh` of `tmp/research-2026-11-16/euler` that it did not start, through a name-filtered PID list (RESULT.md section 9). The sibling waiter (PID 90095) and polling shell (PID 94875) were not touched. This needs an owner decision (restart or clear the stale euler waiters); the learn step took no action on any process. Rule for lane prompts: build PID lists from the lane's own recorded PIDs only, on the Mac as on the server. (b) NOT done by this step, for the planner: device states in `engine/curated/mk_devices.py` (`function-field-order2-vertex` still open, closure proposed for 2 <= h <= 3 on one size and one referee; `effective-order-two` / node `map-open-1` still open while STRATEGY 4.0 says ORDER-2 is closed as an attack lead; lead 17 and lead 4 closures), the engine rerun, the map rows proposed by lanes 1, 3, 6, 7, the Statement O line 3 two-sentence repair and the update of lines 1 and 5 and section C rows 1b, 2a. (c) `scoreboard.py` FRONTIER text was not edited. (d) The repo literature monitor has not logged since 2026-10-22.

- Lead 4, n4 spectral window by reciprocity on (n, c): ledger map A -> A (proved for weight 1; argued for the Motohashi and Blomer-Khan shapes). Reopen only with a reciprocity acting on the Moebius variable l.
- Intersection-form square as the model's crossing device: algebraic in genus 1, upper bound only in genus >= 2.

- "Excess = coherent cross B_coh" as a description of the model excess: not awarded.
- Literature as a source of a crossing input, as of 2026-11-18: none posted.

Slot split (c10 lane 1) + completed-row split (c10 lane 2): one registered exact decomposition of the row energy into principal signal, own-character signal u(m), n = l^2 normaliser term and squarefree-n non-principal part, at a second size ((6, 10, 2) at q = 7, or q = 13) and with seeded classes. ### Lessons

1. All three stage-A candidates for CROSS-1 stopped at the control table, each for a named reason. Reciprocity in the conductor aspect never reads the target's s <-> 1-s. A commutative carrier of periodicity decomposes character by character with the functional equation, so it adds no inequality; the cone leaves the zeta direction unconstrained. Shift-accretivity (de Branges) is false for zeta because it tests bounded zero imbalance at a fixed scale, which the Euler product violates: that device runs against R1.
2. T-NR passes every device whose steps are family positivity plus identities linear in Hecke twists (it is a finite Hecke polynomial at q). T-NR and E_a matrix lifts keep entry positivity, Euler product, functional equation and periodicity together. T-NR joins the stage-A screen for RS-3 lanes.
3. The only devices that no control on file passes are the two periodicity mechanisms (floored 7/8 device, Landau-Siegel device). In degree one the functional equation with integer frequencies forces periodicity (relative to Kaczorowski-Perelli, not opened), so RS-1 and RS-3 admit the same single function; a screen can only use controls that each drop one input.
4. Model (F_q[T]): the probe signal lies where the Hodge form reads reality, not the Weil bound; the completed-row excess is companion energy times a count and is not target-specific. Both open model items (23, 26) are closed against the device. The model has no remaining registered question that carries a flag.
5. Part B: an optimal arbitrary-coefficient sieve cannot move the 7/8 ledgers; its off-diagonal term is already carried (Prop 15.2 exponent 1/6, polar branch b/12, amplifier factor H^(1/n)). Correction to c10 item 1: the window where the bound is not mean-value-optimal is D^(1/2) < K < D^2.
6. Process. (a) Compute every part of a registered statistic that stored data fixes before a heavy run; here it decided the question and showed the second size to be off resonance. (b) Lanes wrote `lanes.jsonl` lines and author-pass REFEREE.md files before the independent referee; the learn step must replace, not append. (c) A control proved relative to a classical theorem keeps the label "relative to" until the primary text is opened (Huxley 1972, Bohr, Hamburger, Kaczorowski-Perelli, Bettin 2016 are all unopened); a numerical enclosure must name its explicit bound.

- Regular-representation matrix bridge, candidates (K), (E), (Q), (H) (commutative decomposition; Perron slice is RH). Not a classification of all cone statements.
- de Branges shift positivity, entire-function and weight forms, and the (a,h) weight family (false for zeta; lattice-zero control passes).
- Model probe as an RS-2 lead in genus >= 2 (symmetric block reads reality only; fakes pass).
- Model item 23: bounded squarefree-n part of the cube term (exceeded at (6,12,2); not target-specific).
- n-th order arbitrary-coefficient large sieve as a part-B input (no figure changes).
- Central-value squares as a device at height t (every control passes).

Device states in `engine/curated` were not edited; closure rows for the three explore devices and map rows G32, Landau-Siegel, P36 are owed to the repair slot.

### Lessons

1. Non-commutativity of a carrier is not read by the states that carry positivity. In Bost-Connes, sigma-invariant states see C(Z^) and vector states return the character split; on X_0(4) the Hecke/Fricke algebra acting on a Fricke-fixed vector collapses to dilations. The round-11 design constraint "non-commutative or non-decomposing carrier" is not sufficient as stated.
2. T-NR is inside each carrier tried: a positive element D >= 1 of C*(Q/Z) (inner perturbation of the Bost-Connes dynamics), a twin of the complex-point multiplier on the oval, and a lift with every input of the cone candidate. A device must fail under multiplication by a positive Dirichlet polynomial at one prime, i.e. it must read exact covariance at every prime (or the size of the Hecke eigenvalue) inside an inequality. No inequality on file consumes this: cone inequalities are preserved by cone endomorphisms.
3. Height and depth do not substitute. The analytic conductor p^k(1+|t|) is a product; integers below N give relations trivial p-adically and non-trivial at the real place once N exceeds about (q|t|)^(1/r). Periodicity mechanisms stay in the conductor aspect; no R4 handle from termwise exchange.
4. A height-uniform local inequality on L-values in the open strip carries no arithmetic (UNIV). The filter is narrow: most devices read the completed function, kernels or coefficients.
5. Weight-form positivity reduces on the boundary to a sign test of Re m on Re s = 1/2 (the outer function drops out); tying characters helps only as a conductor-aspect average (MV-CAP). (N2) holds for forward heat and fails for zeta and backward heat.
6. A structured moment proved as a supremum over bins of count times size squared cannot, by Chebyshev, improve the count it came from. Part-B inputs must be level moments with a witness factor at prime length beyond M1/M2 capacity.
7. Records carry numeric defects that replay finds: unconverged root finding on functions of size 1e-340; "not seen below 1e4" contradicted at zeros 922, 996, 1308, 1496 for (N2_h) at h = 1/2; a step-specific residual in row P43. Second-hand citations can name the right paper and the wrong statement.

### Families now closed (reason)

- Bost-Connes crossed product with Fourier duality, for three classes of states (states see the commutative part; T-NR and E_a pass).
- Depth-aspect termwise exchange of height (Lemma X; no separation of zeta from controls).
- Pointwise open-strip inequalities among finitely many L-values (UNIV); W-adapted cone candidate (CW) (extreme rays with off-line zeros; T-NR lift passes).
- Base-side Hecke/Fricke loading on X_0(4)(R), four listed forms (identity c(pm) = c(m); T-NR twin).
- de Branges shift positivity: backward-heat cell and tied conductor family V3b (false for zeta; boundary sign test).
- Xu-Sang structured prime second moment as a part-B input; frontier slot withdrawn.

Device states in `engine/curated` were not edited. Owed to the repair slot: map rows for lanes 1 to 4 and the Z11/M lines of lane 7; control UNIV from `controls_UNIV_proposed.json`; engine entry from `engine_device_proposal.json`; corrected c11-rs3 DH rows and the P43 figure; independent referees for lanes 1, 2, 4 and Lemmas S, T.

2. Lanes 1, 3, 4 on T-NR: one statement "a device passed by multiplication with a positive Dirichlet polynomial at one prime is passed by T-NR" as a pre-filter (T-NR invariance test) before stage A; with E_a's failing input "theta-normalised kernel with polar terms non-negative" (lane 3 open item) as the one candidate discriminator against E_a.

4. Lane 8 (Bettin: exact reciprocity at height t only for the bilinear pair L(1/2+it,chi-bar)L(1/2+it,chi)) + c11 twist-modulus reciprocity: the bilinear, non-Hermitian form is the only exact reciprocity that reads a height.

1. `c13-kacmoody-eisenstein-affine-weyl-shift` (explore; R4 direct; RS-2). Device: Borel Eisenstein series on the affine Kac-Moody (loop) group of SL_2. The constant term is a sum over the infinite affine Weyl group of ratios xi(<lambda,alpha>)/xi(<lambda,alpha>+1) over real roots alpha_1 + n delta, so the target's own functional equation is applied infinitely often with shifts by the central parameter; every prime enters through the unramified vector. Reason: the only object found in which a group generated by s <-> 1-s and a translation acts on the target itself, which is a transport mechanism from the right of 1; 0 map rows. Expected risk recorded in advance: the Tits-cone condition may keep every argument in Re >= 1.
2. `c13-fourier-interpolation-zero-nodes` (explore; user geometric program: X_0(4) / theta group of `h-klein-fricke`). Device: Fourier interpolation on the theta group (Radchenko-Viazovska) and its form with the zeros of zeta and the points log n as nodes (Bondarenko-Radchenko-Seip). The family of generalised Poisson relations on the nodes sqrt(n) contains zeta (theta) and the control theta^w in one frame. Reason: 0 rows; the explicit formula is one linear relation, the interpolation formula is a basis, and a node set off the real line changes the basis functions' growth, which is the only place an inequality could enter. Risk recorded: the construction may use the functional equation only (DH passes).
3. `c13-lrs-twist-amplification-zero-representation` (explore; RS-1 with RS-2). Device: the Luo-Rudnick-Sarnak mechanism (Rankin-Selberg positivity, twists by characters in the conductor aspect, functional equation whose gamma factor contains the parameter being bounded) applied to the Hecke-eigen object attached to a zero rho: E*(z, rho/2), whose constant term has one exponent exactly because xi(rho) = 0 (Zagier 1981). Reason: the one mechanism on record that turns the functional equation plus periodicity into an inequality on a non-tempered parameter at every place; here the parameter is rho. The step using cuspidality is the first test. E_a is the Eisenstein series at a generic parameter (two exponents); T-NR has no Hecke eigen-object at p_0.
4. `c13-tnr-invariance-prefilter-noncone-census` (combine; round-12 combinable 2). Pair: the T-NR observations of `c12-bost-connes-crossed-product-duality`, `c12-univ-pointwise-control-wcone`, `c12-x04-oval-hecke-fricke-loading` with the E_a kernel condition (lane 3 open item of round 12). Output: one proved pre-filter statement, the 37 device types classified by it, and a census of inequality types not preserved by cone endomorphisms (determinantal, hyperbolicity, norm equalities, entropy) with the T-NR and E_a verdict for each; feeds lane 6 and round 14.
5. `c13-bilinear-reciprocity-height-antibridge` (combine; round-12 combinable 3; user geometric program: bridge / antibridge J-form). Pair: Bettin's exact reciprocity at height t for the bilinear pair L(s,chi)L(s,chi-bar) (self-dual, real coefficients, root number 1) with the twist-modulus reciprocity of `c11-rs1-twist-modulus-reciprocity` and the bilinear (J-form) square of `g-spinor-square-root` / `g-antibridge-cancel`. Reason: the only exact reciprocity on file that reads a height; c11 closed the Hermitian form because it never read a self-dual functional equation.
6. `c13-noncone-kernel-separator-search` (compute, heavy, server). One registered question: does any functional of at most three parameters in a fixed dictionary of non-convex functionals (determinantal minors of orders 2 to 4 of dilation matrices of the theta-normalised kernel with polar terms; Turan-type expressions of the kernel and of its Hecke-compensated forms at primes up to 13) take one sign on zeta and primitive L(s,chi), q <= 13, and the opposite sign on at least one member of every battery control class, stable on held-out members? 1. `c13-kacmoody-eisenstein-affine-weyl-shift` (explore, R4 direct): precise obstruction, score 0. Loop-group SL_2 Borel Eisenstein coefficients are chain products of xi(u)/xi(u+1); in the chamber every denominator argument has Re > 1, strip arguments are numerator zeros only (relative to a recalled normalisation, sources not opened). Rank-one Maass-Selberg positivity is equivalent to a Nevanlinna property and is preserved under Blaschke factors over non-real conjugate pairs (both proved). T-NR (c > 2) and the new control BLS pass. Two-variable affine kernel: closure argued, not proved.
2. `c13-fourier-interpolation-zero-nodes` (explore): precise obstruction, score 0. The Bondarenko-Radchenko-Seip kernel is (F*(s)/F*(w)) times a target-independent factor; bases of one gamma class form one orbit under symmetric multipliers (identity). The Euler product enters only through |mu| <= 1, which stops at Re w = 1. T-NR passes every step (truncation-height step argued); DH and theta^w pass in a smaller class; E_a open at the gamma-class step.
3. `c13-lrs-twist-amplification-zero-representation` (explore): precise obstruction, score 0. For E*(z, rho/2) the Rankin-Selberg function has one simple pole pair at a zero, at 2 - beta; the gamma pole is at 1 - beta, so the difference is 1 for every beta; the forced vanishing is the trivial zero L(0,chi) = 0 of even chi; the inequality is beta <= 7/4. The Eisenstein series at a generic parameter passes every step. LRS exponent count reconstructed, paper not opened. T-NR and DH not formulable.
4. `c13-tnr-invariance-prefilter-noncone-census` (combine): precise obstruction, score 0. Lemma P (proved): T-NR with c > 0 passes every device reading only linear identities, dilation-invariant cone conditions on objects linear in F, and conditions at primes other than p0 (exclusions X1-X5); 13 of 37 device types carry a T-NR record, 0 disagreements. Lemma L (T-NR weights totally positive iff c >= 2) and Lemma E (E_a's xi-kernel inherits every Polya-frequency order). Edrei-Thoma closed as a device. Census of 12 non-cone types: no entry fails both T-NR and E_a at an unconditional input of zeta not passed by another control.
5. `c13-bilinear-reciprocity-height-antibridge` (combine): precise obstruction, score 0. Bettin's reciprocity at height t is Voronoi summation for E(z, 1/2+it): the height is a spectral parameter with arguments tied at integer offsets. J-form entries are real with sign (-1)^(zero count in the window), fixed by the argument principle whether or not the zeros are on the line. T-NR multiplies every entry by a positive number; for E_a every entry is a Hermitian square. Referee corrected the decay rate of the remainder integrand (Lemma 1 stands).
6. `c13-noncone-kernel-separator-search` (compute, heavy): precise obstruction (outcome "none", MEASURED, finite search), score 0. 12,994,697 functionals of dictionary D (minors of orders 2-4, Turan-type expressions, Hecke-compensated forms, with and without polar terms) on 290 objects: none is one-signed on zeta and the characters q <= 13 and opposite on a member of every control class; 0 of 629,432 one-signed functionals reject battery DH, 400 reject backward heat. The positive condition does not hold out (q 14-40 opposite at mean rate 0.287). Registered prediction "T-NR passes D1" false (0.646); "E_a passes D1" holds. Lemma H proved for order 2.
7. `c13-repair-map-engine-battery-records` (repair): partial result, score 0. c11-rs3 screen recomputed in balls (31 of 54 minima, 2 of 54 fail counts change; no verdict changes); battery D1 20 of 20 against the corrected record (not a blind test); HEAT tau = -0.5 list completed to 294 pairs (measured); map 186 -> 191 rows; engine: control UNIV and three closed device types (39 closed, 1 floored, 0 open). Corrected files sit in the lane only.
8. `c13-referee-r12-independent` (repair): partial result, score 0. Independent second referee of five round-12 statements: all hold, four in a narrower form (Lemma X needs |t| >= t_r; Bost-Connes statements for the Galois-invariant KMS state only; the oval T-NR twin has c < -2 with zeros outside the strip; Lemmas S, T given Conrey-Li Theorem 2; UNIV relative to a survey statement, U4 argued). Eight labels to restrict or downgrade.

### Lessons

1. Scattering carriers: positivity of truncated Eisenstein norms acts on denominators xi(u+1) at Re >= 1; strip arguments are zeros of scattering coefficients and the inequality is closed under inner (Blaschke) factors. Pre-filter for any scattering device: does the inequality survive multiplication of the coefficient by a Blaschke product over non-real conjugate pairs (control BLS; T-NR with c > 2 is one).
2. An object built "from the zeros" (interpolation basis with zero nodes, Eisenstein series at rho/2) is a universal object of the gamma class divided by the target or evaluated at a parameter. The target enters linearly, so symmetric multipliers with strip zeros pass (lane 2), and a zero supplies the number of poles but not their position (lane 3: pole at 1 plus the parameter in every rank).
3. Lemma P is now a written pre-filter: linear identities, dilation-invariant cone conditions and conditions away from p0 are passed by T-NR. T-NR and E_a are separated by complementary kernels (xi-kernel translation determinants cannot fail E_a; theta-kernel conditions of order 1-2 with the constant term cannot fail T-NR), and no single-object sign functional up to order 4 fails both without also failing L-functions or passing DH (lanes 4, 6).
4. A bilinear pair on the critical line reads the zero count mod 2, not the location; exact reciprocity reads height as a spectral parameter with tied arguments (lane 5). With c11 (Hermitian form) this closes exact reciprocity as a height-reading device in both forms.
5. The measured search confirmed the registered "no survivor" outcome but refuted one registered sub-prediction (T-NR does not pass D1): local total positivity of a coefficient sequence says nothing about dilation minors of the kernel. Float64 was wrong where polar terms cancel; ball arithmetic decided 841,651 cases.
6. Records: a root finder that stops on absolute size is unconverged where a completed L-function is small; a zero list is complete only by a box count. One-line summaries of closures drop restrictions (round-12 twin, KMS state, UNIV class N) that later filters depend on; the second referee restored them.

### Families now closed (reason)

- Loop-group / Kac-Moody Borel Eisenstein series, rank-one proved, affine kernel argued (denominators stay at Re > 1; Maass-Selberg positivity closed under Blaschke factors; T-NR and BLS pass).

Birkhoff-polytope and permanent inequalities on the regular-representation series M_q: E_a has coefficient matrices that are non-negative multiples of permutation matrices, so the normalised sum is doubly stochastic for E_a too (cone only). Hadamard-Fischer on M_q* M_q: equals the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality over characters. Residual (square-integrable) Eisenstein classes on larger groups with unitarity of the residual spectrum: poles in the positive chamber come from numerators at 1; zeros of zeta enter only as denominators at Re >= 1 (round-13 lesson 1). Product kernel of zeta(s)L(s,chi_D) as a sum of class theta series: Epstein zeta functions of non-principal classes have positive kernels and off-line zeros (`r2-classgroup-homotopy`, `n2-epstein-degree-nine-anchors`). Conrey-Bettin reciprocity for the twisted second moment as the Hermitian pair at height t: closed in both forms (c11, round-13 lane 5). Bohr-Jessen value distribution in 1/2 < sigma < 1 with Baker-type quantitative independence of log p: a statement for almost every t (MV-CAP). Resurgence of the Riemann-Siegel expansion, isomonodromic tau functions, inverse scattering for the bridge S: coefficient-blind or restatements in the sense of map section 2 (DH has the same gamma side; the Marchenko condition is the Krein index of `sq-krein-index`).
