### Lessons

1. Reading zeta together with its twists does not supply the missing input. For HEAT, BLS and T-NR the twist commutes with the control's defining operation (lane 1, Lemma J1); the twist relation is a property of the character, so a pair functional that consumes it exactly cancels the prime side and leaves an archimedean form (lane 2, identity TW); ratio functionals are blind to common multipliers (Lemma C).
2. Sign and size compose only formally: in Lemma A (lane 2) the comparison link between a window count of zeta and of its twists is equivalent to the target.
3. A zero in the strip changes a Rankin-Selberg pole pattern only through a constant-term coefficient whose argument is in the strip, never through a Fourier coefficient and never right of 1 (lanes 3 and 6). In rank one, and for every torus parameter and every representation r, pole minus local pole equals 1. The template "zero supplies cuspidality, a theorem contradicts it" is the classical proof on Re s = 1 and stops there.
4. A norm built from the Hecke algebra on the resonance channel is a wave-growth norm; positivity-based (solid) norms force the constants into the space and stop at the edge (lane 4).
5. In the user geometric program every node read is an identity that a control passes, the target or a certificate, or carries no condition on a function class (lane 5; one reader's typing, referee does not hold on one PROVED sentence).
6. Closing the devices that tested a read set does not close the read set (lane 8): the record speaks only where a control lies inside the class. For {FE, GEOM}, {FE, SIZE} and identification classes no control on file has zeta's own functional equation, so R3 cannot be tested there. [Correction 2026-11-19, `c16-repair-owed-record-r15` (restriction 30 of `push/c15-referee-r14-independent-checkpoint/LABEL_RESTRICTIONS.md`): "no control on file has zeta's own functional equation" is false as written (T-NR in symmetric form has it); read "no control on file has zeta's own functional equation together with an ordinary Dirichlet series (INT)". Checkpoint to use: `push/c15-referee-r14-independent-checkpoint/CHECKPOINT_R15_REFEREED.md` (U1, U2 as cells of U3 with INT in the read set; U4, U6 (a), (b), (g), U8 narrowed).]
7. Process: referee first-pass numerics were wrong three times through truncated series (lane 7): a second check with a different truncation before reporting a sign pattern. A lane must write outputs only inside its own directory (lane 4 incident). Rerunning merge_outcomes.py rewrites learn-step entries.

### Families now closed (reason)

- Joint sign functionals of a kernel pair (F, F x chi), homogeneous in each kernel, dictionary DJ (measured, finite search; twist commutes with HEAT, BLS, T-NR; no functional rejects all nine control classes).
- Ratio / winding functionals of L(chi)/zeta (blind to common multipliers; the comparison step is the target), cross bridge Gram of a pair (identities for any unitary pair; BLS passes), twist-transfer Weil forms (prime sides cancel; DH, T-NR, E_a pass).
- LRS-type count on a rank-one eigen-object at a zero, including the weight-1/2 metaplectic Eisenstein series (pole minus gamma pole equals 1; generic parameter passes).
- Lax-Phillips resonance states in solid, Hecke-sup, L^q and Hecke-invariant zero-mode norms (bound is the definition, admissibility, or Zagier's criterion).
- Catalogued X2 inequalities on a torus-parameter eigen-object (Lemma T; every bound below 1/2 needs L2, pole at 1, spectral membership or unitarity).
- User geometric-program nodes as sources of a new inequality (sweep by one reader; two nodes without a map row by name remain).

Owed: independent referees for lanes 1 to 6 and for `CHECKPOINT_R14_DRAFT.md`; repair of sentence E1 in lane 5; the 13 label restrictions of lane 7; map rows and device entries of lanes 2, 3, 4, 6 (MAP_ROW.md, engine_device_proposal.json, proposed row G37, control NR-vec); BLS and UNIV in CONTROL_CODE_RE of engine/build_graph.py. Device states in `engine/curated` were not edited by the learn step.

1. Lemma C (lane 2) + dictionary DJ (lane 1): the T-NR pair class should include primes with chi(p0) = 1 for every character in the dictionary; with Lemma J1 this gives a paper pre-filter for any future pair functional (homogeneous in each kernel: closed without a search).

3. Lane 6's list of proved inequalities with a zero-specific hypothesis and none of L2 / pole-at-1 / spectral membership / unitarity (Maass-Selberg G31, Lax-Phillips contraction G26, the edge theorem) + lane 4 (d): all three reach Re rho <= 1 only; a quantitative version of the edge argument (Proposition E with a strip width) is the one shape not typed as closed.

### Lessons

1. An amplifier cannot improve an inequality whose two sides are one explicit vector with exactly known norm (lane 1). On truncated Eisenstein data Hecke operators act only by dilating the truncation height (same structure as rows G26, D9). The quantitative edge argument loses in inputs outside the norm (sieve lower bound, sup|zeta'|) and stays weaker than the classical region.
2. With zeta's functional equation fixed, the family is zeta times a symmetric multiplier (lanes 2, 7). Small-defect controls are absolutely continuous (rational multipliers, defect O(eta^2 + d^2)); discrete ones sit at l1 distance >= 1. What identifies zeta is the functional equation together with an ordinary Dirichlet series and the pole clause (Lemma L). "No control on file has zeta's own functional equation" was false: T-NR in symmetric form has it.
3. For window devices the number of windows is not the controlling variable (lane 3): against translates only the relative spread of heights counts (L/T >= eta log(T/2pi)/pi); adjacent windows cost about 0.36 real conditions each.
4. A tapered section makes the limit step from finite stages sound, and thereby moves the whole target into the stage inequality at unbounded P (lane 4). Band-by-band square-root bounds do not separate zeta from random sign chains; the one-sided sum over all bands does, and that is the Weil form.
5. Quotients and logarithms do not escape the Gaussian-multiplier blindness (lane 5): any read unchanged by a common pointwise factor is passed by backward heat exactly. Lift-dependence of an outcome "none" is decidable by a leave-one-class-out recount on stored sign matrices (lane 7).
6. A zero sum weighted by T^(c d) with c > 1 is a pointwise statement above abscissa 1/c; a window certificate needing R <= pi/(4M) is not sign-definite at height when M >= c log t (lane 6).
7. Process: two lanes wrote `checks.json` to the repo root through relative paths; an untracked root file of that name was overwritten and is not recoverable (lanes 2, 6). Scripts must write next to themselves. One lane polled server logs at 20-60 s (brief says minutes). A spectral formula derived at a zero is not a check at a hypothetical delta (lane 1). Rerun engine and scoreboard before editing the record (lane 8).

- Hecke amplification of the truncated Eisenstein norm at a zero, T >= N (the norm is exact; amplified inequality implied by the unamplified one; EIS-w, BLS pass).
- Read classes U1 {FE, GEOM} and U2-SIZE as separate untestable classes (control ZQ passes on atoms; on coefficients they are cells of U3).
- U5 window devices within one span, and identification-type conclusions against translates (translate passes T^a windows for every a < 1; free-prime products pass below a parameter-count crossover).
- U7 growing sections P = t^theta of the prime chain (Fejer-tapered stage inequality is row P45, the target; sharp sections expansive).
- Non-homogeneous functionals of kernel pairs and triples, dictionary DN, nine-class and six-class tiers (measured; HEAT blind to quotient reads exactly). The canonical three-class tier is not closed (67,138 undecided nodes).
- The pair klein-phase-transport-certificate + lemma-w-wide-support (Lemma W is target-level; the certificate is not sign-definite at height).

### Lessons

1. Rank 2 adds the reflection of left-half zeros to rank one, which is the functional equation of the zero set and nothing more (lane 1). Symmetric Blaschke insertions keep f(z) = f(1-z); the BLS row's "lacks f(z) = f(1-z)" is wrong for symmetric zero sets.
2. With zeta's functional equation, Lambda >= 0 acts only through N >= 0, and N >= 0 with no mass below frequency 1 is Poisson rigidity (lane 2). {FE, POS} identifies zeta without the Euler product and says nothing about zeros; its quantitative form is the target. Fully fractional LP relaxations are uninformative (minimum falls like 1/K).
3. A positivity statement at P = t^theta reads only frequencies <= theta log t of the zero measure; its reach grows linearly with the local number of off-line pairs and is not a zero-free region (lane 3). One-pair heuristics overstate what such devices exclude.
4. ZQ passes finite certificates and atom-only steps inside zeta's own functional-equation class; a critical-line step must be discontinuous in the coefficient measure at zeta (margin zero, reach at least 0.48 sqrt(eps) in sup norm) (lane 4). Sign searches on compact parameter sets sit inside the flow-blindness wall: Lemma H fixes the HEAT column of a polynomial functional from zeta's own values (lanes 4, 5, 7).
5. Below the truncation height Hecke operators stop acting by dilation, but the effect is a bounded constant shared by EIS-w (lane 6). A pre-registered decision clause needs the control comparison written into it.
6. Process: balls need a relative acceptance test (an absolute floor counted 256-bit balls containing 0 as decided); a hold-out read at one threshold hides nodes that pass at another; A-only families need a full character set (lane 5). Entering read classes as open device nodes changes the generator's ranking (lane 8). Lanes still cannot start their own referee; `ctheta` held 9 to 10 cores for about 12 minutes; the server was overloaded during lanes 5 and 7.

### Families now closed (reason)

- U2-POS / Lemma POS-FE: closed as an identification row in U3 (Theorem P, three referees); the quantitative form is target-level.
- Proposition 5 / C(theta) as a zero-side region for P = t^theta devices (lattice rows invisible in the window; clusters bounded only by c n/(theta log gamma); no multiplicativity).
- Dictionary DN canonical tier {T-NR, E_a, HEAT} and DJ canonical lifts (MEASURED, outcome "none"; 0 undecided). Not re-read in balls: FE tier, quotient minors, product families.
- U6 (g) at rank 2 in the form "Gram positivity read off the zero set" (symmetric Blaschke insertions pass; measured, closure not proved).
- The 71 silent geometric-program nodes as explore candidates (target-level or passed by ZQ or a recorded control; one reader).
- Family 31 item "amplifier with T < N": measured obstruction on T <= 4, N <= 128 (not a proved closure).

Owed: map rows for lanes 1 to 6 (U6 (g) device row with the restricted BLS-right row and the corrected BLS row; U2-POS identification row; Q30/P45 addendum; G41 update: canonical tier closed as MEASURED; family 31 note); STRATEGY 4.0 entry for POS-FE; the eleven corrected sentences of lane 7 (two in STRATEGY section 0b); planner decision on the eleven "open" silent-cell device states before the top 60 is used; disposition of the fired S2 clause of lane 6; second referee for Lemma H before any U8 run; source checks (rank-2 inner product formula and range of T; Turan's second main theorem); second referees for lanes 1, 3, 4, 5, 6. Device states in `engine/curated` were not edited by the learn step.

### Results that could be combined

The combined question is the shape of the discontinuity: which functional of the coefficient measure is 0 on r zeta and bounded below by a multiple of sqrt(eps) on the ZQ family, and whether a multiplicative input (N a sum over integers with multiplicative weights) makes it one-sided. Any such statement is target-level in quantitative form, so the lane must name the step that fails for ZQ as defect tends to 0 and for NR-q at every lift value.

Lemma R (lane 1) + ZQ's f(u) = f(1-u) + Maass-Selberg positivity for ZQ (open, lane 4): decides whether rank-2 Gram positivity is closed under symmetric Blaschke factors (proof closes U6 (g) as a row; a counterexample at order 10 to 20 would be the first inequality ZQ fails on a scattering carrier).

### Lessons

1. Positivity of a state is invariant under conjugation by an invertible element, so twins of the form b xi (T-NR, finite DEL, gauge) can be rejected only by an exact relation (lane 1). For beta <= 1 product-vector states are orthogonal: no function of tau remains to read a zero.
2. For a Hecke eigenform with a product formula, the sum and the product of the values at the isogenous points are dilation-module quantities; reads built from mean and norm are pointwise functions of dilates and Epstein forms pass them at inert primes (lane 2).
3. A quantitative identification ("defect delta implies zeros within f(delta)") on a class containing zeta at defect 0 is the target. What is provable without the target is stability, which reads the functional equation and integrality, not multiplicativity. NR-q is multiplicative on its semigroup, so no functional excludes it through multiplicativity (lane 3). The sqrt(eps) reach is the discriminant of a function real on the line.
4. The rank-2 Gram form is rank-one Maass-Selberg plus one apex kernel at T = 0, which reads the reflection a -> 1-a of left zeros and needs the pole at 1 in the base; it does not read the critical line (lane 4).
5. The reach of a band-limited positivity device is a density condition, with two structural bands (P = sqrt(t/2pi) count-preserving, P = t/2pi exactness). Multiplicativity is present exactly at every band for every multiplicative chain; the missing input is a one-sided operator bound tied to the functional-equation reserve (lane 5).
6. Process: a scanned-threshold hold-out read can be passed by an identification, so register a minimum relative threshold margin; label float64 exclusions as float64; exact construction of symmetric zero sets (float pairing gave false failures at 1e-17); check the sign of a residue before using Lemma R at a second pole; with cells out of the device pool the generator cannot name the next explore slot (lanes 4, 6, 7, 8). Server gave about 0.9 core to an 8-core unit in lane 6 and lane 4 ran 47% of its plan.

- U6 (f), norm / Jensen gap / discriminant of the isogenous points (dilation-module identities; Epstein passes at inert primes). One referee. Residual: symmetric functions of degree >= 3, no candidate inequality.
- U3 as an inequality (target-level in three relaxation directions; Proposition W and Lemma LR). One referee.
- U8 on dictionary DU with the three canonical lifts (MEASURED, outcome "none", 4,360,000 family-nodes with the referee's completion).
- C(theta) / U5 window devices as a zero-free region (window information law; replaces rows S12b, Q30, P45 addendum once refereed a second time).
- Not closed: row D10 / cell U6 (g) (tried, not closed; inertia law for zeta open).

Owed: independent referees for lanes 1, 2, 3, 5 (lane 5 with the Turan primary source, Fejer-Riesz for exponential type and the explicit formula in the operator normalisation); map rows: D8 (vi) for U6 (e), G33 wording for U6 (f), U3-as-inequality target-level with the NR-q note, the U8 row of lane 6 RESULT section 8, the window-law row, D10 revision (ZQ rational form passes; DH-base note); nine sentence corrections in round-16 lane files; G42 restriction; the unconstrained LP at X = 4000 (optional); Montgomery chapter 5 for the range of nu.

### Lessons

- Both proposed device types of round 18 are obstructed on one referee each. `exact-relation-rigidity`: rigidity on file is linear algebra in the coefficient measure (FE, integrality, pole clause); multiplicativity is linear in log G and is never needed; the clause that completes the identification is analyticity on the critical line, the only one not stated right of 1. `tz-one-sided-completion-bound-band`: below the Nyquist band N(theta) holds for ZQ and for sign chains (no R3) and is a statement about zeros of a partial sum (no R1); at theta' = 1 it is the target (no R4); the linear reflected completion is not a positive extension.
- Three-way split for inequalities left of 1 (lane 1): a convergent test of the sign of Lambda, or a pointwise bound equal to the target. Atom-by-atom positivity carries no R1.
- Controls must be run on their own zero configuration (E_a passes the band device for small a on zeta's ordinates; the lattice-model row was wrong as a general statement). T-NR is rejected by log-level devices only through a coefficient sign, and truth of an inequality at random points does not separate it.
- A device that reads positivity of a kernel with base (u+1)/(u-1) reads the pole and the reflection symmetry of the zero set: symmetric off-line quartets pass (proved in the model). Row D10 cannot score whatever X(d) gives.
- Unique Whittaker model forces Gauss sums (nothing beyond Z14/Z15); non-unique model gives global period data without a Hecke relation. Neither gives an inequality and zeta controls do not lift.
- Process: the server shares CPU per unit, so split work into single-thread units; one job was refused under load and ran locally; `$S:path` expands as a zsh modifier (use `${H}:path`); a proposed node's state must change on a referee file, not on entry; mode-count dependence must be reported with any window measurement.

Owed (next record lane): set `exact-relation-rigidity` and `tz-one-sided-completion-bound-band` to closed in `engine/curated/devices.json` (this learn step does not edit device states); map rows X1 + control HQ2, the tz row of lane 2 RESULT section 9, U4 (iv) row, Z24 (MAP_ROW.md), Q31 text with two referees; correction of display (A) in lane 2; Proposition D (c) of lane 6; restate the c17 parity clause with b = u(u-1)g; second referees for lanes 1, 2, 3, 4, 6; the five runs of `push/c18-tz-one-sided-bound-fe-reserve-bridge/FOR_NUMERICS_LANE.md` (not done); Montgomery's Ten Lectures for the factor 2 in Turan's theorem; referee for W5c.

### Lessons

- Band device family (N(theta), W, C(theta), every completion tried: linear reflected, Riemann-Siegel sign-change) is closed on four refereed lanes. A completion positive by construction moves all content into the defect; the one-sided read sees an off-line quartet only through a pair form that vanishes with eta, so ZQ passes; the two-sided read is the target in the window. No step reads multiplicativity.
- Zeros of partial sums D_P are closed as a carrier. At fixed P every height-uniform statement is shared by all unimodular chains (K1) and by twins that differ above P (K3); at P = t/2pi the zero set is a function of zeta through the approximate functional equation, so ZQ shares it; in between zeta is inside the Haar ensemble. Completion C1 is false for every class.
- Cell U4 has one read left: a second-order form mixing A_P with the archimedean operator. Cross-prime sign relations exist as a failing step for T-NR and sign chains (the first such step on file), but the read set "all atoms in phase" contains E_a and DEL.
- HQ2 screen: nothing on file reads multiplicativity jointly with the functional equation inside the strip. Local Euler-shape reads reject one-prime twins by identification, in the same class as the pole clause; local positivity does not (HQ3, HQ4). Controls HQ3, HQ4 should enter the control set.
- Cell U6 (c) is closed (no product structure, no sign, Eisenstein and cusp not separated). After this round the only cell without a passing control is the U4 second-order residual.
- Row D10 is a model-level open step (NL(d)); it cannot score.
- Process: two lanes carried a wrong statement that a control passes or that a step is PROVED (a check at P = 10 with a single cross atom; a positivity claim for every point set). Control rows need a case large enough to contain the first failing atom, and PROVED positivity statements need their domain stated. Ball products by a unit complex ball inflate radii about 1.4x per step. Survivor rules must give each control zeta's number of windows. Margins from window measurements depend on mode count; signs did not.

Owed (next record lane): map rows from MAP_ROW.md of lanes 3, 5, 6 (Z37, D10, HQ3/HQ4 control rows and engine control codes); correction E1 of lane 1 in its RESULT.md, map row and lane line; repaired Lemma P of lane 2; the 23 corrected sentences of lane 7 RESULT section 3 (Q31 with Theorem K.3); device state for the band device after lane 2; second referees for Lemma Q2, K1-K3, Lemmas N, S, F, C, Run A certification, the interchange lemma of lane 5, Lemma 1 and Theorems 2-4 of lane 6; an enclosed tail bound for Theorem A; round-20 checkpoint items of `CHECKPOINT_R20_INPUT.md`. Server: 2.1 GB of lane-2 files remain in `~/rh-push/c19-rs-real-zero-completion-band-server/`; no unit reported running.

2. Lane 3 + round-18 Lemma Q: Euler-shape rigidity without the pole clause for infinitely many modified primes (extend Lemma F to sets with sum p^(-1/2) finite; kill test: a twin with geometric local factors and zeta's functional equation). An identification and a pre-filter, not a route to zeros.
3. Lane 6 NL(d) + lane 7 confirmation of Theorem Q: proof of NL(d) closes row D10 in the model sense (record value only).
4. Lane 2 successor: a lower bound for |D_N| on the line at N = sqrt(t/2pi) from the prime side would bound the defect; aimed at the two-sided statement it is the target itself.

No lane passed stage A and nothing on file has a registered reason to expect R4. Lessons.

- Cell U4 is closed at every read tried (first order, quadratic, joint phase, second-order mixed). Reason: in a window the archimedean side is a scalar up to log t/t, and any form reading Lambda(n), n < P, with the gamma side is identical for twins modified at q >= P. No stage-level inequality at P = t^theta' locates zeros near t. No further attack slot.
- The completed second-order form T2 is closed as a device: zeta fails it at theta' = 0.95 and DEL passes. Forms whose sign follows the size of the prime sum favour deletion products over zeta.
- Cells U6 (a) linear and U6 (b) are closed: rank >= 2 non-Borel objects read a zero only as a removed constant-term exponent, and the first object reading zeta in the strip is a BLS member. Residue: the regularised truncated norm of the degenerate P_(2,1) series (row D10 form), and the double cover (argued).
- Finite Pick orders on bounded node ranges are closed as a discriminator: Theorem C gives a non-Euler series with off-line zeros passing any order N. Open only for unbounded node range.
- Zero-free finite stages are not an arithmetic property (Lemma E). A flag "multiplicativity essential" needs a non-multiplicative control failing the named step and no multiplicative control failing it. The c1 flag is withdrawn under that test; the c4 flag has not been put to it.
- Combinable: (i) Theorem A of lane 4 (rigidity in the summable class) with K3-M of lane 1: twins modified at q >= P are invisible to stage forms, and twins with summable modification do not exist in the functional-equation class, so the only twin class left for both is the boundary regime sum |1-a(p)| p^-sigma divergent for some sigma > 1/2. (ii) Lemma T and the enclosed Theorem A limit (lane 2) with the certified heat-zero enclosure method (lane 6) give a certified pipeline for any future zero-side identity. (iii) Theorem C (lane 5) with Lemma E (lane 7): two constructions of non-multiplicative objects passing stage-level tests, usable as a screen before any new stage device is opened.
- Least blocked: the boundary regime of lane 4 (relative product convergent only for Re s > 1/2: natural boundary or a twin). It is the one place where an R4-shaped statement (passage to the line) is posed in relative form with a proof method on file; it is an identification question and by itself scores 0. Second: a referee test of the restricted R1 of `c4-helson-discrepancy-zerofree` against Lemma E and the HQ controls, since the headline now rests on it. Lessons.

- Headline stays 0 of 4. No lane this round entered a requirement flag; the two prefilter lanes (3, 4) produced no object for the round-23 inverse-explore slot, so that slot has no filtered candidate.
- Regime (K), dense remainder: closed for phases +, for periodic phases with non-zero beta_2, and for almost every phase assignment on super-sparse fibres. All three are natural-boundary rows that hold for the integer model lambda_k = k and for any base: frequency-blind and base-blind, not R1. This is the second refereed obstruction of the same kind on BR-2 (round 21: Theorem W, P0), so the round-22 stopping rule closes regime (K) as a source of a requirement flag. What stays open is one identification question: a non-periodic deterministic phase or sign assignment on dense S, reduced by Lemma TR to growth of prod(1 - c_k w^k) beyond log^2 near every arc.
- In the dense remainder the boundary is made by the product over all harmonics of a local factor, not by independence of log q: the first harmonic alone continues. This corrects the round-21 lesson, which holds on the sparse side only.
- Finite statistics do not see primes: "prime nearest exp(f(k))" behaves as the Beurling sequence f(k), and a flag certifies telescoping only to relative frequency error 1e-10 to 1e-8. Diagonal dominance is not a route to the missing-lemma integral (k^(1/2): off-diagonal cancels the diagonal to 1e-7 while M stays above O(V)). A registered height set is to be checked against the positive control's pole geometry before the run.
- BR-3': with zeta's functional equation and integer frequencies, rigidity is Liouville / Phragmen-Lindelof and needs growth on one sequence of crossings only; nothing reads the Euler product except an identification inside the countable Artin class. Close BR-3' as a lead unless a tame twin is built from Lemma M or the Fourier-support-on-squares formulation is verified.
- Input D: a step linear in f that reads the order of the integers is passed by the V6 twin f * w; a step that characterises complete multiplicativity does not read the order and is passed by every sign chain and every Beurling member. A causal multiplier does not factor through a Hankel operator, so all-n relations bound Hankel norms only right of 1. Uncovered form: a step non-linear in f that reads the order (S_f(x)S_f(y) against S_f(xy)).
- Archimedean-side classes: either strip exponents are admitted (ZQ, SG are members) or the functional equation is pinned and Hamburger returns zeta without the axes. Height averages, densities, limsups and periodic parts of a divisor are unchanged by finite divisor modifications. Two prefilter lines to enter (after a referee of Lemma A1).
- U4 control table is now certified: thin and dense deletions (b = 0.55 to 0.9) make the band form negative at explicit windows; zeta stays positive on 2.0e9 screened heights (float64 screen, not a statement at all heights). A non-uniform FFT height scan plus a separate ball evaluation decides such rows at about 19x lower cost.
- Families closed this round: BR-2 as a flag source (both sides); BR-3 and, as a lead, BR-3'; TS section 6 row 4 (input D joins, for the joins written); axis support / crystalline-measure classification; non-relative Weyl independence.
- Combinable: (i) Lemma TR (transfer to the integer-frequency model, any phases) + Proposition PC (Polya-Carlson) + Theorem R (null set): a deterministic non-periodic sign assignment either has a rational generating function (periodic, decided by E2 / E3) or the unit circle is a natural boundary of sum c_k w^k; the missing step is the passage from that boundary to growth of the product.
